The wide range of climates leads to a high demand for water. Over time, [droughts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Droughts_in_California) have been increasing [due to climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_California) and [overextraction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_extraction),[^cite_note-145] becoming less seasonal and more year-round, further straining California's [electricity supply](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electricity_supply)[^cite_note-146] and [water security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_security)[^cite_note-147][^cite_note-148] and having an impact on California business, industry, and agriculture.[^cite_note-149]&#x20;

In 2022, a new state program was created in collaboration with [Indigenous peoples of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_California) to revive the practice of [controlled burns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controlled_burn) as a way of clearing excessive forest debris and making landscapes more resilient to wildfires. [Native American use of fire in ecosystem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_American_use_of_fire_in_ecosystems) management was outlawed in 1911, yet has now been recognized.[^cite_note-Elassar-2022-15]&#x20;

### Ecology

Main articles: [Ecology of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecology_of_California) and [Environment of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environment_of_California)



[Mount Whitney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Whitney), in the Sierra Nevada, is the highest mountain in the [contiguous U.S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contiguous_U.S.)

California is one of the ecologically richest and most diverse parts of the world, and includes some of the most endangered ecological communities. California is part of the [Nearctic realm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nearctic_realm) and spans a number of terrestrial [ecoregions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecoregion).[^cite_note-The_California_Invasive_Species_List-150]&#x20;

California's large number of [endemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemic_\(ecology\)) species includes [relict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relict_\(biology\)) species, which have died out elsewhere, such as the Catalina ironwood (*[Lyonothamnus floribundus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyonothamnus_floribundus)*). Many other endemics originated through differentiation or [adaptive radiation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adaptive_radiation), whereby multiple species develop from a common ancestor to take advantage of diverse ecological conditions such as the California lilac (*[Ceanothus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceanothus)*). Many California endemics have become endangered, as urbanization, logging, [overgrazing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overgrazing), and the introduction of [exotic species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exotic_species) have encroached on their habitat.&#x20;

### Flora and fauna

Main articles: [Fauna of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fauna_of_California) and [California Floristic Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Floristic_Province)

See also: [List of California native plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_California_native_plants)



Yosemite's [Grizzly Giant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grizzly_Giant), a 209' tall giant sequoia, Earth's [largest tree species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_trees).

[^cite_note-151]

&#x20;Listed as endangered by the [IUCN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IUCN), most [sequoia groves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_giant_sequoia_groves) have been granted protected status.

[^cite_note-152]



Originally utilized by [Indigenous peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_California) as a source of food and fiber for textiles, the western Joshua Tree (*[Yucca brevifolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yucca_brevifolia)*) is a recognizable native plant of Californian deserts and protected under the State's *Western Joshua Tree Conservation Act* of 2023.

[^cite_note-153]

[^cite_note-154]

[^cite_note-155]

California boasts several superlatives in its collection of flora: the [largest trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giant_sequoia), the [tallest trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coast_redwood), and the [oldest trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinus_longaeva). California's native grasses are [perennial plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perennial_plant),[^cite_note-Restoring_Native_Grasses-156] and there are close to hundred succulent species native to the state.[^cite_note-157] After European contact, these were generally replaced by [invasive species](67554.md) of European annual grasses; and, in modern times, California's hills turn a characteristic golden-brown in summer.[^cite_note-The_California_Invasive_Species_List-150]&#x20;

Because California has the greatest diversity of climate and terrain, the state has six life zones which are the lower [Sonoran Desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonoran_Desert); upper Sonoran (foothill regions and some coastal lands), transition (coastal areas and moist northeastern counties); and the Canadian, Hudsonian, and Arctic Zones, comprising the state's highest elevations.[^cite_note-California:_flora_and_fauna-158]&#x20;

Plant life in the dry climate of the lower Sonoran zone contains a diversity of native cactus, mesquite, and paloverde. The [Joshua tree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yucca_brevifolia) is found in the Mojave Desert. Flowering plants include the dwarf desert poppy and a variety of [asters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aster_\(genus\)). [Fremont cottonwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fremont_cottonwood) and [valley oak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valley_oak) thrive in the Central Valley. The upper Sonoran zone includes the chaparral belt, characterized by forests of small shrubs, stunted trees, and herbaceous plants. *[Nemophila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nemophila)*, [mint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamiaceae), *[Phacelia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phacelia)*, *[Viola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viola_\(plant\))*, and the California poppy (*[Eschscholzia californica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eschscholzia_californica)*, the state flower) also flourish in this zone, along with the lupine, more species of which occur here than anywhere else in the world.[^cite_note-California:_flora_and_fauna-158]&#x20;

The transition zone includes most of California's forests with the redwood (*[Sequoia sempervirens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sequoia_sempervirens)*) and the "big tree" or giant sequoia (*[Sequoiadendron giganteum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sequoiadendron_giganteum)*), among the oldest living things on earth (some are said to have lived at least 4,000 years). [Tanbark oak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanbark_oak), [California laurel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_laurel), [sugar pine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugar_pine), [madrona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arbutus), [broad-leaved maple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acer_macrophyllum), and [Douglas-fir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas-fir) also grow here. Forest floors are covered with [swordfern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polystichum), alumnroot, [barrenwort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barrenwort), and [trillium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trillium), and there are thickets of [huckleberry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huckleberry), [azalea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azalea), elder, and wild currant. Characteristic wild flowers include varieties of mariposa, [tulip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulip), and [tiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lilium_columbianum) and [leopard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lilium_pardalinum) lilies.[^cite_note-Sequoia_sempervirens_(D._Don)_Endl.-159]&#x20;



A [California sea lion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_sea_lion) in [La Jolla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Jolla)



A [Tule Elk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tule_Elk) in the [San Joaquin Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Joaquin_Valley)

The high elevations of the Canadian zone allow the [Jeffrey pine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_pine), [red fir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_fir), and [lodgepole pine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lodgepole_pine) to thrive. Brushy areas are abundant with dwarf [manzanita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manzanita) and ceanothus; the unique [Sierra puffball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calvatia_sculpta) is also found here. Right below the timberline, in the Hudsonian zone, the whitebark, foxtail, and silver pines grow. At about 10,500 feet (3,200 m), begins the Arctic zone, a treeless region whose flora include a number of wildflowers, including [Sierra primrose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primula_suffrutescens), [yellow columbine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquilegia_flavescens), [alpine buttercup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranunculus), and [alpine shooting star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dodecatheon_alpinum).[^cite_note-California:_flora_and_fauna-158][^cite_note-Life_Zones_of_the_Central_Sierra_Nevada-160]&#x20;

[Palm trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm_trees) are a well-known feature of California, particularly in [Southern California](62520.md) and [Los Angeles](18110.md); many species have been imported, though the *Washington filifera* (commonly known as the *California fan palm*) is native to the state, mainly growing in the [Colorado Desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Desert) oases.[^cite_note-161] Other common plants that have been introduced to the state include the [eucalyptus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eucalyptus), [acacia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acacia), [pepper tree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schinus), geranium, and [Scotch broom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotch_broom). The species that are federally classified as endangered are the [Contra Costa wallflower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erysimum_capitatum), [Antioch Dunes evening primrose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oenothera_deltoides_subsp._howellii), [Solano grass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuctoria_mucronata), [San Clemente Island larkspur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphinium_variegatum), [salt marsh bird's beak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordylanthus_maritimus), [McDonald's rock-cress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabis_blepharophylla), and [Santa Barbara Island liveforever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dudleya_traskiae). As of December 1997, 85 plant species were listed as threatened or endangered.[^cite_note-California:_flora_and_fauna-158]&#x20;

In the deserts of the lower Sonoran zone, the mammals include the [jackrabbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jackrabbit), [kangaroo rat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kangaroo_rat), squirrel, and opossum. Common birds include the [owl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Owl), [Greater roadrunner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_roadrunner), [cactus wren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cactus_wren), and various species of hawk. The area's reptilian life include the [sidewinder viper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crotalus_cerastes), [desert tortoise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desert_tortoise), and [horned toad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horned_toad). The upper Sonoran zone boasts mammals such as the [antelope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pronghorn), [brown-footed woodrat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dusky-footed_woodrat), and [ring-tailed cat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ring-tailed_cat). Birds unique to this zone are the [California thrasher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_thrasher), [bushtit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psaltriparus_minimus), and [California condor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_condor).[^cite_note-California:_flora_and_fauna-158][^cite_note-California_Condor-162][^cite_note-CalPhotos:_Browse_Mammal_Common_Names-163][^cite_note-Quail_Ridge_Reserve:_UC_Davis_Natural_Reserve_System-164]&#x20;

In the transition zone, there are Colombian [black-tailed deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black-tailed_deer), [black bears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_black_bear), [gray foxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gray_fox), [cougars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cougar), [bobcats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobcat), and [Roosevelt elk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roosevelt_elk). Reptiles such as the garter snakes and rattlesnakes inhabit the zone. In addition, amphibians such as the [water puppy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proteidae) and [redwood salamander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batrachoseps_attenuatus) are common too. Birds such as the [Belted kingfisher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belted_kingfisher), chickadee, [towhee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Towhee), and hummingbird thrive here as well.[^cite_note-California:_flora_and_fauna-158][^cite_note-Black-tailed_Deer_of_California-165]&#x20;

The Canadian zone mammals include the [Long-tailed weasel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long-tailed_weasel), [snowshoe hare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snowshoe_hare), and several species of chipmunks. Conspicuous birds include the [blue-fronted jay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steller%27s_jay), [mountain chickadee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_chickadee), [hermit thrush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermit_thrush), [American dipper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_dipper), and [Townsend's solitaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Townsend%27s_solitaire). As one ascends into the Hudsonian zone, birds become scarcer. While the [gray-crowned rosy finch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gray-crowned_rosy_finch) is the only bird native to the high Arctic region, other bird species such as [Anna's hummingbird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna%27s_hummingbird) and [Clark's nutcracker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clark%27s_nutcracker). Principal mammals found in this region include the [American pika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_pika), [white-tailed jackrabbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White-tailed_jackrabbit), and the [bighorn sheep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bighorn_sheep). As of April 2003, the bighorn sheep was listed as endangered by the U.S. Fish and Wildlife Service. The fauna found throughout several zones are the [mule deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mule_deer), [coyote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coyote), [mountain lion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_lion), [northern flicker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_flicker), and several species of hawk and sparrow.[^cite_note-California:_flora_and_fauna-158]&#x20;



[Methuselah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methuselah_\(pine_tree\)) is the [oldest tree in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_oldest_trees), found in the [Ancient Bristlecone Pine Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Bristlecone_Pine_Forest) of [Inyo National Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inyo_National_Forest).

Aquatic life in California thrives, from the state's mountain lakes and streams to the rocky Pacific coastline. Numerous trout species are found, among them [rainbow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rainbow_trout), [golden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_trout), and [cutthroat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cutthroat_trout). Migratory species of salmon are common as well. Deep-sea life forms include [sea bass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_seabass), [yellowfin tuna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellowfin_tuna), [Pacific barracuda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_barracuda), and several types of whale. Native to the cliffs of northern California are [seals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harbor_seal), [California sea lion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_sea_lion), and many types of shorebirds, including migratory species.[^cite_note-California:_flora_and_fauna-158]&#x20;

As of April 2003, 118 California animals were on the federal endangered list; 181 plants were listed as endangered or threatened. Endangered animals include the [San Joaquin kitfox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vulpes_macrotis), [Point Arena mountain beaver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aplodontia_rufa_nigra), [Pacific pocket mouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_pocket_mouse), [salt marsh harvest mouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salt_marsh_harvest_mouse), [Morro Bay kangaroo rat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morro_Bay_kangaroo_rat) (and five other species of kangaroo rat), [Amargosa vole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_vole#Subspecies), [California least tern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_least_tern), [California condor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_condor), [loggerhead shrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loggerhead_shrike), [San Clemente sage sparrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell%27s_sparrow), [San Francisco garter snake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_garter_snake), five species of salamander, three species of chub, and two species of pupfish. Eleven butterflies are also endangered[^cite_note-166] and two that are threatened are on the federal list.[^cite_note-167][^cite_note-168] Among threatened animals are the coastal [California gnatcatcher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_gnatcatcher), [Paiute cutthroat trout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paiute_cutthroat_trout), [southern sea otter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_otter#Subspecies), and [northern spotted owl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_spotted_owl). California has a total of 290,821 acres (1,176.91 km<sup>2</sup>) of National Wildlife Refuges.[^cite_note-California:_flora_and_fauna-158] As of September 2010, 123 California animals were listed as either endangered or threatened on the [federal list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Fish_%26_Wildlife_Service).[^cite_note-U.S._Fish_&_Wildlife_Service:_Species_Reports:_Listings_and_occurrences_for_California-169] Also, as of the same year, 178 species of California plants were listed either as endangered or threatened on this federal list.[^cite_note-U.S._Fish_&_Wildlife_Service:_Species_Reports:_Listings_and_occurrences_for_California-169] Multiple mammal species have been extirpated from the state in recent times including [brown bear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_bear), [jaguar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaguar), and [American bison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_bison).&#x20;

### Rivers

Main article: [List of rivers of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rivers_of_California)



[San Francisco Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Bay) (center left) and the [California Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacramento%E2%80%93San_Joaquin_River_Delta) (top right)

The most prominent river system within California is formed by the Sacramento River and [San Joaquin River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Joaquin_River), which are fed mostly by snowmelt from the west slope of the Sierra Nevada, and respectively drain the north and south halves of the Central Valley. The two rivers join in the [Sacramento–San Joaquin River Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacramento%E2%80%93San_Joaquin_River_Delta), flowing into the Pacific Ocean through [San Francisco Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Bay). Many major tributaries feed into the Sacramento–San Joaquin system, including the [Pit River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pit_River), [Feather River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feather_River) and [Tuolumne River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuolumne_River).&#x20;

The [Klamath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klamath_River) and [Trinity Rivers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinity_River_\(California\)) drain a large area in far northwestern California. The [Eel River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eel_River_\(California\)) and [Salinas River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salinas_River_\(California\)) each drain portions of the California coast, north and south of San Francisco Bay, respectively. The [Mojave River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mojave_River) is the primary watercourse in the Mojave Desert, and the [Santa Ana River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Ana_River) drains much of the [Transverse Ranges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transverse_Ranges) as it bisects Southern California. The [Colorado River](180968.md) forms the state's southeast border with Arizona.&#x20;

Most of California's major rivers are dammed as part of two massive water projects: the [Central Valley Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Valley_Project), providing water for agriculture in the Central Valley, and the [California State Water Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Water_Project) diverting water from Northern to Southern California. The state's coasts, rivers, and other bodies of water are regulated by the [California Coastal Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Coastal_Commission).&#x20;

### Regions

Main article: [List of regions of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_regions_of_California)

See also: [List of places in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_places_in_California)



A map of [California's regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_regions_of_California)



A map showing the division between [Northern California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_California) (top white) and [Southern California](62520.md) (bottom red)

California is traditionally separated into [Northern California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_California) and [Southern California](62520.md), divided by a straight border which runs across the state, separating the northern 48 counties from the southern 10 counties. Despite the persistence of the northern-southern divide, California is more precisely divided into many regions, multiple of which stretch across the northern-southern divide.&#x20;

- **[Southern California](62520.md)**

### Cities and towns

See also: [List of cities and towns in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_California) and [List of largest California cities by population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_California_cities_by_population)

California has 483 [incorporated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_corporation) cities and towns,[^cite_note-483rd_city-170] of which 461 are cities and 22 are towns. Under California law, the terms "city" and "town" are explicitly interchangeable. The name of an incorporated [municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality) in California can either be "City of (Name)" or "Town of (Name)".[^cite_note-Cal_Gov_Code_34502-171]&#x20;

[Sacramento](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacramento,_California) became California's first incorporated city on February 27, 1850.[^cite_note-Sacramento_Charter-172] [San Jose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jose,_California), [San Diego](28504.md), and [Benicia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benicia,_California) tied for California's second incorporated city, each receiving incorporation on March 27, 1850.[^cite_note-San_Jose_Inc-173][^cite_note-History_of_San_Diego-174][^cite_note-Benicia_Inc-175] [Mountain House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_House,_California) became the state's most recent and 483rd incorporated municipality on July 1, 2024.[^cite_note-483rd_city-170] The majority of these cities and towns are within one of five [metropolitan areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_area): the [Los Angeles Metropolitan Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Metropolitan_Area), the [San Francisco Bay Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Bay_Area), the [Riverside-San Bernardino Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riverside-San_Bernardino_Area), the [San Diego metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Diego_metropolitan_area), or the [Sacramento metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacramento_metropolitan_area).&#x20;

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_California)

### Population

Studies indicate that the Native American population in California in 1850 was close to 150,000 before declining to 15,000 by 1900.[^cite_note-americanindiantah1-182] |  |  |  |

Presently, close to one out of every nine U.S. residents live in California.[^cite_note-PPIC-183][^cite_note-40Mill-184] According to the [2020 U.S. census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_U.S._census), the population of California was 39.54 million on April 1, 2020, a 6.13% increase since [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_census).[^cite_note-Census2020-180] During that decade, the state's population grew more slowly than the rest of the nation, resulting in the loss of one seat on the U.S. House of Representatives, the first loss in its entire history.[^cite_note-PPIC-183] The estimated state population in 2023 was 38.94 million.[^cite_note-40Mill-184] For well over a century (1900–2020), California experienced steady population growth. Even while the rate of growth began to slow by the 1990s, some growth progressed into the first two decades of the 21st century; California added an average of around 400,000 people per year to its population during the period 1940–2020.[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-Byler-186][^cite_note-187] Then in 2020, the state began to experience population declines continuing every year, attributable mostly to moves out of state but also due to declining [birth rates](515898.md), [COVID-19 pandemic deaths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_deaths), and less internal migration from other states to California.[^cite_note-PPIC-183][^cite_note-188] According to the U.S. Census Bureau, between 2021 and 2022, 818,000 California residents moved out of state[^cite_note-189] with emigrants listing high cost of living, [California housing shortage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_housing_shortage)[^cite_note-190] rise of remote work,[^cite_note-191] high taxes, and a difficult business environment as the motivation. The net loss of population in California between July 2020 and July 2023 was 433,000.[^cite_note-PPIC-183] As of the 2020 Census, California had a [population density](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_density) of 250/mi<sup>2</sup> and a [population-weighted density](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population-weighted_density) of 7954/mi<sup>2</sup>.[^cite_note-192][^cite_note-193]&#x20;



California's population density, 2020

The [Greater Los Angeles Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Los_Angeles_Area) is the second-largest [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_area) in the U.S., while Los Angeles is the [second-largest city in the U.S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_cities_by_population) [Los Angeles County](79734.md) has held the title of most populous U.S. county for decades and it alone is more populous than 42 U.S. states.[^cite_note-LA_County_DPSS-194][^cite_note-Baby_Slump_In_L.A._County-195] San Francisco is the most densely populated city in California and [one of the most densely populated cities in the U.S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_cities_by_population_density). Four of the top 20 most populous cities in the U.S. are in California: Los Angeles (2nd), [San Diego](28504.md) (8th), [San Jose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jose,_California) (13th), and San Francisco (17th). The [center of population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_of_population) of California is located four miles west-southwest of the city of [Shafter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shafter,_California), [Kern County](80557.md).[^cite_note-197]&#x20;

As of 2020, California ranked [fourth among states by life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_life_expectancy), with a life expectancy of 79.0 years.[^cite_note-198]&#x20;

Starting in 2010, for the first time since the [California Gold Rush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Gold_Rush), California-born residents made up the majority of the state's population.[^cite_note-199] Along with the rest of the U.S., California's immigration pattern has also shifted over the course of the late 2000s to early 2010s.[^cite_note-Pew_Mexican_Immigration-200] Immigration from [Latin American](18524.md) countries has dropped significantly with most immigrants now coming from Asia.[^cite_note-SacBee17JAN2013-201] In total for 2011, there were 277,304 immigrants; 57% came from Asian countries versus 22% from Latin American countries.[^cite_note-SacBee17JAN2013-201] [Net immigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_immigration) from Mexico, previously the most common country of origin for new immigrants, has dropped to zero / less than zero since more Mexican nationals are departing for their home country than immigrating.[^cite_note-Pew_Mexican_Immigration-200]&#x20;

The state's population of [undocumented immigrants](7124412.md) has been shrinking in recent years, due to increased enforcement and decreased job opportunities for lower-skilled workers.[^cite_note-202] The number of migrants arrested attempting to cross the [Mexican border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borders_of_Mexico) in the Southwest decreased from a high of 1.1 million in 2005 to 367,000 in 2011.[^cite_note-BusinessWeek-203] Despite these recent trends, undocumented immigrants constituted an estimated 7.3% of the state's population, the third highest percentage of any state in the [country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illegal_immigrant_population_of_the_United_States),[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205] totaling nearly 2.6 million.[^cite_note-SDUT19042011-206] In particular, illegal immigrants tended to be concentrated in [Los Angeles](79734.md), [Monterey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monterey_County,_California), [San Benito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Benito_County,_California), [Imperial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_County,_California), and [Napa Counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napa_County,_California)—the latter four of which have significant agricultural industries that depend on manual labor.[^cite_note-PPIC2011II-207] More than half of illegal immigrants originate from Mexico.[^cite_note-SDUT19042011-206] The state of California and some California cities, including [Los Angeles](18110.md), [Oakland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oakland), and [San Francisco](49728.md),[^cite_note-208] have adopted [sanctuary policies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctuary_city).[^cite_note-209]&#x20;

According to [HUD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Housing_and_Urban_Development)'s 2022 [Annual Homeless Assessment Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annual_Homeless_Assessment_Report_to_Congress), there were an estimated 171,521 [homeless](19394651.md) people [in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness_in_California).[^cite_note-210][^cite_note-211]&#x20;

According to the U.S. Census Bureau, in 2022 the population self-identified as (alone or in combination): 56.5% [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Americans) (including [Hispanic Whites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic_White_Americans)),[^cite_note-220] 33.7% [non-Hispanic white](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Hispanic_whites),[^cite_note-221] 18.1% [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_American),[^cite_note-222] 7.3% Black or [African American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_American),[^cite_note-223] 3.2% [Native American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States) and [Alaska Native](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Native),[^cite_note-224] 0.9% [Native Hawaiian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Hawaiian) or [Pacific Islander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islander_American),[^cite_note-225] and 34.3% [some other race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Some_Other_Race).[^cite_note-226] These numbers add up to more than 100% because respondents can select multiple racial identities. 19% of Californians identified as two or more races in 2022, although excluding respondents who selected "some other race", only 5% identified as two or more races.[^cite_note-227]&#x20;

By ethnicity, in 2018 the population was 60.7% non-Hispanic (of any race) and 39.3% [Hispanic or Latino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanics_and_Latinos_in_California) (of any race). Hispanics are the largest single ethnic group in California.[^cite_note-2018CensusDemo-228] [Non-Hispanic whites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Hispanic_whites) constituted 36.8% of the state's population.[^cite_note-2018CensusDemo-228] *[Californios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Californio)* are the [Hispanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic) residents native to California, who make up the [Spanish-speaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_language_in_California) community that has existed in California since 1542, of varying [Mexican American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_American)/[Chicano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicano), [Criollo Spaniard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criollo_people), and Mestizo origin.[^cite_note-229] However, they make up only a small part of California's Hispanic population today, estimated at 500,000. California has the largest Mexican, [Salvadoran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvadoran_Americans), and [Guatemalan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guatemalan_Americans) populations in the U.S., together making up over 90% of the state's Latino population.[^cite_note-230]&#x20;

According to 2022 estimates from the [American Community Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Community_Survey), 32.4% of the population had [Mexican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Americans) ancestry, 6.6% had [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Americans) ancestry, 6.1% had [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Americans) ancestry, 5.6% had [Irish](775859.md) ancestry, 4.9% had [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Americans) ancestry, 4.3% had [Filipino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_Americans) ancestry, 4% had [Central American](6121.md) ancestry (Mostly [Salvadoran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvadoran_Americans) and [Guatemalan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guatemalan_Americans)), 3.4% had [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Americans) ancestry, 2.8% listed themselves as [American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_ancestry), and 2.5% had [Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Americans) ancestry.[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233]&#x20;



Largest alone or in any combination ethnic origin by county in California, per the 2020 census



Ethnic origins in California

As of 2011, 75.1% of California's population younger than age 1 were minorities, meaning they had at least one parent who was not non-Hispanic white (white Hispanics are counted as minorities).[^cite_note-234]&#x20;

In terms of total numbers, California has the largest population of White Americans in the U.S., an estimated 22,200,000 residents including people identifying as white in combination with any other race. The state has the 5th largest population of African Americans in the U.S., an estimated 2,250,000 residents. California's Asian American population is estimated at 7.1 million, constituting a third of the nation's total. California's Native American population of 504,000 is the most of any state, with 103,030 identifying as Non-Hispanic and belonging mostly to the [Indigenous peoples of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_California).[^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236] Most of the state's Native American population identifies as Hispanic and belongs to Indigenous Mexican or Central American ethnic groups, including 185,200 identifying as [Mexican American Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_Mexico) and 67,904 identifying as [Central American Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_american_indians).[^cite_note-237]&#x20;

According to estimates from 2011, California has the largest [minority population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majority-minority_state) in the U.S. by numbers, making up 60% of the state population.[^cite_note-CensusQuickFacts-238] Over the past 25 years, the population of [non-Hispanic whites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Hispanic_whites) has declined, while [Hispanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic_and_Latino_American) and [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_American) populations have grown. Between 1970 and 2011, non-Hispanic whites declined from 80% of the state's population to 40%, while Hispanics grew from 32% in 2000 to 38% in 2011.[^cite_note-239] It is currently projected that Hispanics will rise to 49% of the population by 2060, primarily due to domestic births rather than immigration.[^cite_note-Economist_CA_Hispanics-240] With the decline of immigration from Latin America, Asian Americans now constitute the fastest growing racial/ethnic group in California; this growth is primarily driven by immigration from [China](5405.md), [India](14533.md), and the [Philippines](23440.md), respectively.[^cite_note-Asians_CA-241]&#x20;

The majority of California's immigrants were born in Latin America (49%) or Asia (41%). Most of California's immigrant population were born in Mexico (3.8 million), the Philippines (821,136), China (795,450), India (574,498), and Vietnam (525,455) and more than half (55%) of California's immigrants were naturalized US citizens in 2023 which has increased consistently since 1990, when only 31% of immigrants were naturalized.[^cite_note-242]&#x20;

### Languages

See also: [California English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_English) and [Spanish language in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_language_in_California)

[English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_English) serves as California's [de jure](43594.md) and [de facto](43594.md) [official language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_language). According to the 2021 [American Community Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Community_Survey) conducted by the U.S. Census Bureau, 56.08% (20,763,638) of California residents age 5 and older spoke only [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language) at home, while 43.92% spoke another language at home. Some 60.35% of California residents who speak a language other than English at home are able to speak English "well" or "very well", with this figure varying significantly across the different linguistic groups.[^cite_note-Language2021-243] Like most U.S. states (32 out of 50), California law enshrines English as its official language, and has done so since the passage of [Proposition 63](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986_California_Proposition_63) by California voters in 1986. Various government agencies do, and are often required to, furnish documents in the various languages needed to reach their intended audiences.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-AB_800_Analysis-245][^cite_note-Hull_English_already_official-246]&#x20;

[Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_language_in_California) is the most commonly spoken language in California behind English, spoken by 28.18% (10,434,308) of the state's population in 2021.[^cite_note-Language2021-243] The Spanish language has been spoken in California since 1542 and is deeply intertwined with California's cultural landscape and history.[^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249] Spanish was the official administrative language of California through the Spanish and Mexican eras, until 1848. Following the U.S. [Conquest of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conquest_of_California) and the [Treaty of Guadalupe-Hidalgo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Guadalupe-Hidalgo), the U.S. federal government guaranteed the rights of Spanish-speaking Californians. The first [Constitution of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_California) was written in both languages at the [Monterey Constitutional Convention of 1849](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Constitutional_Conventions) and protected the rights of Spanish speakers to use their language in government proceedings and mandating that all government documents be published in both English and Spanish.[^cite_note-250]&#x20;





The [Constitution of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_California) was written in both [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_English) (left) and [Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_language_in_California) (right) by both American and [Californio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Californio) delegates.

Despite the initial recognition of Spanish by early American governments in California, the revised 1879 state constitution stripped the rights of Spanish speakers and the official status of Spanish.[^cite_note-251] The growth of the [English-only movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English-only_movement) by the mid-20th century led to the passage of [1986 California Proposition 63](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986_California_Proposition_63), which enshrined English as the only official language in California and ended Spanish as a language of instruction in public schools.[^cite_note-Time_Bilingual-252] [2016 California Proposition 58](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_California_Proposition_58) reversed the prohibition on [bilingual education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilingual_education), though there are still many barriers to the proliferation of Spanish bilingual education, including a shortage of teachers and lack of funding.[^cite_note-253][^cite_note-Time_Bilingual-252][^cite_note-254] The [government of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_California) has since made efforts to promote Spanish language access and bilingual education,[^cite_note-255][^cite_note-256] as have private educational institutions in California.[^cite_note-257] Many businesses in California promote Spanish language skills for their employees, to better serve both California's Hispanic population and the larger [Spanish-speaking world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanophone).[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259]&#x20;

California has historically been one of the most linguistically diverse areas in the world, with more than 70 Indigenous languages derived from 64 root languages in six language families.[^cite_note-Native_Languages_1770-260][^cite_note-Indian_Root_Languages_1994-261] A survey conducted between 2007 and 2009 identified 23 different Indigenous languages among California farmworkers.[^cite_note-262] All of California's Indigenous languages are [endangered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endangered_language), although there are now efforts toward [language revitalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_revitalization).[^cite_note-263] California has the highest concentration nationwide of [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language_in_the_United_States), [Vietnamese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_language_in_the_United_States) and [Punjabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjabi_American) speakers.&#x20;

As a result of the state's increasing diversity and migration from other areas across the country and around the globe, linguists began noticing a noteworthy set of emerging characteristics of spoken [American English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_English) in California since the late 20th century. This variety, known as [California English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_English), has a [vowel shift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vowel_shift) and several other phonological processes that are different from varieties of American English used in other regions of the U.S.[^cite_note-Bucholtz-264]&#x20;

### Religion

See also: [List of cathedrals in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cathedrals_in_California)

Religious self-identification, per [Public Religion Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Religion_Research_Institute)'s 2021 *American Values Survey*

[^cite_note-265]

1. [Catholicism](410868.md) (34.0%)

2. [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism_in_the_United_States) (27.0%)

3. [Jehovah's Witness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses) (1.00%)

4. [Mormonism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mormonism) (1.00%)

5. [Unaffiliated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion_in_the_United_States) (28.0%)

6. [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_the_United_States) (2.00%)

7. [Judaism](1131183.md) (1.00%)

8. [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_the_United_States) (1.00%)

9. Other (5.00%)

The largest [religious denominations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_denomination) by number of adherents as a percentage of California's population in 2014 were the Catholic Church with 28%, Evangelical Protestants with 20%, and Mainline Protestants with 10%. Together, all kinds of Protestants accounted for 32%. Those unaffiliated with any religion represented 27% of the population. The breakdown of other religions is 1% Muslim, 2% Hindu and 2% Buddhist.[^cite_note-pew2014-266] This is a change from 2008, when the population identified their religion with the Catholic Church with 31%; Evangelical Protestants with 18%; and Mainline Protestants with 14%. In 2008, those unaffiliated with any religion represented 21% of the population. The breakdown of other religions in 2008 was 0.5% Muslim, 1% Hindu and 2% Buddhist.[^cite_note-267] The *[American Jewish Year Book](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Jewish_Year_Book)* placed the total [Jewish](1131183.md) population of California at about 1,194,190 in 2006.[^cite_note-268] According to the [Association of Religion Data Archives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Religion_Data_Archives) (ARDA) the largest denominations by adherents in 2010 were the [Catholic Church](606848.md) with 10,233,334; [The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints_in_California) with 763,818; and the [Southern Baptist Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Baptist_Convention) with 489,953.\[^cite_note-[www.thearda.com-269](http://www.thearda.com-269/)]&#x20;



The [Cathedral of San Carlos Borromeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_San_Carlos_Borromeo_\(Monterey,_California\)) in Monterey, built 1791–94, is the oldest [parish](23623.md) in California.

[^cite_note-270]

California has a large Catholic population due to the large numbers of Mexicans and Central Americans living within its borders. California has twelve dioceses and two archdioceses, the [Archdiocese of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Los_Angeles) and the [Archdiocese of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocese_of_San_Francisco), the former being the largest archdiocese in the U.S.&#x20;

A [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center) survey revealed that California is somewhat less religious than the rest of the states: 62% of Californians say they are "absolutely certain" of their belief in God, while in the nation 71% say so. The survey also revealed 48% of Californians say religion is "very important", compared to 56% nationally.[^cite_note-Helfland_2008-271]&#x20;

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_California)

See also: [Cuisine of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_California)



The [Hollywood Sign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Sign) in [Los Angeles](18110.md)

The culture of California is a [Western culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_culture) and has its modern roots in [U.S. culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._culture), but also, historically, many [Hispanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic) [Californio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Californio) and [Mexican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Mexico) influences. As a border and coastal state, California culture has been greatly influenced by several large immigrant populations, especially those from [Latin America](18524.md) and [Asia](689.md).[^cite_note-World_of_Opportunity-272]&#x20;

California has long been a subject of interest in the public mind and has often been promoted by its boosters as a kind of paradise. In the early 20th century, fueled by the efforts of state, the building projects during the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression) and local boosters, many Americans saw the Golden State as an ideal resort destination, sunny and dry all year round with easy access to the ocean and mountains. In the 1960s, popular music groups such as [the Beach Boys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Beach_Boys) promoted the image of Californians as laid-back, tanned beach-goers.&#x20;

### Motion picture entertainment

Further information: [Media in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Los_Angeles), [Media in the San Francisco Bay Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_the_San_Francisco_Bay_Area), and [Music in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_in_California)

See also: [List of amusement parks in the Americas § California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_amusement_parks_in_the_Americas#California), [List of newspapers in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_newspapers_in_California), and [List of radio stations in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_radio_stations_in_California)



[Disney](37398.md), headquartered in [Burbank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burbank,_California), is one of the world's largest media and entertainment companies.

[Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood,_Los_Angeles) and the rest of the Los Angeles area is a major global center for motion picture entertainment, with the [U.S. film industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_in_the_United_States)'s ["Big Five" major film studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_film_studio) ([Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_Pictures), [Disney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walt_Disney_Pictures), [Paramount](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Pictures), [Universal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Pictures), and [Warner Bros.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Bros._Pictures)) as well as many minor film studios being based in or around the area. Many [animation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animation) studios are also headquartered in the state. The four major American television commercial broadcast networks ([ABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Broadcasting_Company), [CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS), [NBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC), and [Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Broadcasting_Company)) as well as other networks all have production facilities and offices in the state. All the four major commercial broadcast networks, plus the two major Spanish-language networks ([Telemundo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telemundo) and [Univision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Univision)) each have at least three [owned-and-operated TV stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Owned-and-operated_television_stations_in_the_United_States) in California, including at least one in Los Angeles and at least one in San Francisco.[^cite_note-273]&#x20;



[Google](1092923.md), headquartered in [Mountain View](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_View,_California), is one of the largest [technology companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technology_companies) in the world.



The [California State Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Fair) is held annually during the summer at [Cal Expo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cal_Expo) in northeastern [Sacramento](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacramento).

One of the oldest radio stations in the U.S. still in existence, [KCBS (AM)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KCBS_\(AM\)) in the [San Francisco Bay Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_the_San_Francisco_Bay_Area), was founded in 1909. [Universal Music Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Music_Group), one of the "[Big Four](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_market)" record labels, is based in [Santa Monica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Monica,_California), while [Warner Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Records) is based in [Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles,_California). Many independent record labels, such as [Mind of a Genius Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mind_of_a_Genius_Records), are also headquartered in the state. California is also the birthplace of several international music genres, including the [Bakersfield sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bakersfield_sound), [Bay Area thrash metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_Area_thrash_metal), [alternative rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_rock), [g-funk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G-funk), [nu metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nu_metal), [glam metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glam_metal), [thrash metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thrash_metal), [psychedelic rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychedelic_rock), [stoner rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stoner_rock), [punk rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punk_rock), [hardcore punk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hardcore_punk), [metalcore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metalcore), [pop punk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_punk), [surf music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surf_music), [third wave ska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_wave_ska), [west coast hip hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_coast_hip_hop), [west coast jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_coast_jazz), [jazz rap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz_rap), and many other genres. Other genres such as [pop rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_rock), [indie rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indie_rock), [hard rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_rock), [hip hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip_hop), [pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_music), [rock](25423.md), [rockabilly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockabilly), [country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_music), [heavy metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heavy_metal_music), [grunge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grunge), [new wave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_wave_music) and [disco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disco) were popularized in the state. In addition, many British bands, such as [Led Zeppelin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Led_Zeppelin), [Deep Purple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_Purple), [Black Sabbath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Sabbath), and [the Rolling Stones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Rolling_Stones) settled in the state after becoming internationally famous.&#x20;

As the home of [Silicon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Valley), the Bay Area is the headquarters of several prominent [internet media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_media), [social media](5897742.md), and other technology companies. Three of the ["Big Five" technology companies](57174306.md) ([Apple](856.md), [Meta](62420226.md), and [Google](1092923.md)) are based in the area as well as other services such as [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix), [Pandora Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandora_Radio), [Twitter](9988187.md), [Yahoo!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahoo!), and [YouTube](3524766.md). Other prominent companies that are headquartered here include [HP inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HP_inc.) and [Intel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intel). [Microsoft](19001.md) and [Amazon](90451.md) also have offices in the area.&#x20;

### Car culture

[Car culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Car_culture) in California, particularly [Southern California](62520.md),[^cite_note-274] is considered the birthplace of modern [car culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1950s_American_automobile_culture).[^cite_note-275]&#x20;

### Food

Main article: \[\[:[California Cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_cuisine)]]

Several [fast food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fast_food), [fast casual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fast_casual_restaurant), and [casual dining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_casual_dining_restaurant_chains) chains were also founded California, including some that have since expanded internationally like [California Pizza Kitchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Pizza_Kitchen), [Denny's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denny%27s), [IHOP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IHOP), [McDonald's](2480627.md), [Panda Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panda_Express), and [Taco Bell](37563.md).&#x20;

### Sports

Main article: [Sports in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_California)

See also: [List of professional sports teams in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_professional_sports_teams_in_California)



[Pebble Beach Golf Links](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pebble_Beach_Golf_Links), one of the best ranked [golf courses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golf_course) in the world



The [Grand Prix of Long Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Prix_of_Long_Beach) is the longest running major [street race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Street_race) in North America.

California has 19 [major professional sports league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_professional_sports_leagues_in_the_United_States_and_Canada) franchises, far more than any other state. The [San Francisco Bay Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Bay_Area) has five major league teams, while the [Greater Los Angeles Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Los_Angeles_Area) is home to ten. San Diego has two major league teams and Sacramento has two. The NFL [Super Bowl](27718.md) has been hosted in California 12 times at five different stadiums: [Los Angeles Memorial Coliseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Memorial_Coliseum), the Rose Bowl, Stanford Stadium, [Levi's Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levi%27s_Stadium), and [San Diego Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Diego_Stadium). A thirteenth, [Super Bowl LVI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LVI), was held at [SoFi Stadium](45525802.md) in [Inglewood](107640.md) on February 13, 2022.[^cite_note-276]&#x20;

California has long had many respected collegiate sports programs. California is home to the oldest college bowl game, the annual [Rose Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose_Bowl_Game), among others.&#x20;

The [NFL](21211.md) has three teams in the state: the [Los Angeles Rams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Rams), [Los Angeles Chargers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Chargers), and [San Francisco 49ers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_49ers).&#x20;

[MLB](38776.md) has five teams in the state: the [San Francisco Giants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Giants), [Los Angeles Dodgers](18213.md), [Los Angeles Angels](1360083.md), [San Diego Padres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Diego_Padres), and the [Athletics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athletics_\(baseball\)), which plan to move to [Las Vegas](47737.md) by 2028.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278]&#x20;

The [NBA](22093.md) has four teams in the state: the [Golden State Warriors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_State_Warriors), [Los Angeles Clippers](72892.md), [Los Angeles Lakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Lakers), and [Sacramento Kings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacramento_Kings). Additionally, the [WNBA](57721.md) also has two teams in the state: the [Los Angeles Sparks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Sparks) and [Golden State Valkyries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_State_Valkyries).&#x20;

The [NHL](21809.md) has three teams in the state: the [Anaheim Ducks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anaheim_Ducks), [Los Angeles Kings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Kings), and [San Jose Sharks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jose_Sharks).&#x20;

[MLS](71802.md) has four teams in the state: the [Los Angeles Galaxy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Galaxy), [San Jose Earthquakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jose_Earthquakes), [Los Angeles FC](44255607.md), and [San Diego FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Diego_FC), while [NWSL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Women%27s_Soccer_League) has three teams in California: [Angel City FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angel_City_FC), [Bay FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_FC), and [San Diego Wave FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Diego_Wave_FC).&#x20;

[MLR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_League_Rugby) has one team in the state: the [San Diego Legion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Diego_Legion).&#x20;

California is the only U.S. state to have hosted both the [Summer](27566.md) and [Winter](34069.md) Olympics. The [1932](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1932_Summer_Olympics) and [1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Summer_Olympics) summer games were held in [Los Angeles](18110.md). Squaw Valley Ski Resort (now [Palisades Tahoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palisades_Tahoe)) in the Lake Tahoe region hosted the [1960 Winter Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1960_Winter_Olympics). Los Angeles will host the [2028 Summer Olympics](12695048.md), marking the fourth time that California will have hosted the Olympic Games.[^cite_note-279]&#x20;

Multiple games during the [1994 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup) took place in California, with the [Rose Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose_Bowl_Stadium) hosting eight matches (including the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup_Final)), while [Stanford Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanford_Stadium) hosted six matches.&#x20;

[SoFi Stadium](45525802.md) in Los Angeles hosted eight matches during the [2026 World Cup](36581929.md), including five group stage fixtures, two Round of 32 clashes, and a Quarter-Final.[^cite_note-280] [Levi's Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levi%27s_Stadium) in [Santa Clara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Clara,_California) hosted six matches, including five group stage matches, and the Round of 32 match between the [USMNT](582488.md) and [Bosnia and Herzegovina](1008974.md).&#x20;

In addition to the Olympic games, California also hosts the [California State Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Games).&#x20;

Many sports, such as [surfing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surfing), [snowboarding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snowboarding), and [skateboarding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skateboarding), were invented in California, while others like [volleyball](32558.md), [beach soccer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beach_soccer), and [skiing](28478.md) were popularized in the state.&#x20;

Other sports that are big in the state include [golf](19568112.md), [rodeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodeo), [tennis](29773.md), [mountain climbing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_climbing), [marathon running](49132.md), [horse racing](74711.md), [bowling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bowling), [mixed martial arts](228344.md), [boxing](4243.md), and [motorsports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorsports), especially [NASCAR](21114.md) and [Formula One](10854.md).&#x20;



[Los Angeles Memorial Coliseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Memorial_Coliseum) hosted the [Summer Olympics](27566.md) in [1932](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1932_Summer_Olympics) and [1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Summer_Olympics), and will also host in [2028](12695048.md).
